## U21 · NOTICE · 2026-10-01 · session_01TNeXM2Qvi7zMXT6BntbENE · primary
DEC-112 (Bob, question 30): a published case comes in three forms. The public page: each finding opens with one line naming its role and the project's bar, never a bare "meets", then its grades. The complete edition: a self-contained file anyone can read, check and use to recreate the case without CivicOS, no length limit, with "How to check this case yourself" and the grading method. The case file: a published, versioned format holding the complete edition, everything a conclusion rests on (whole, with extracted text), chains, attestations, signatures and the method version inside the signed case; split, never trimmed; an open specification and a standalone open checker. Everything a conclusion rests on must be presentable: publication is refused while a relied-on finding rests on material that cannot travel whole. Off-the-record material travels whole with its attestations; only the identity is withheld. Import lands in a new read-only project; the system confirms each finding by recreating it (Recreated / Recreated in part / Did not recreate); acceptance only on recreated findings.
Folded: BIO_Publication_v0_1.md §5C, §9; BIO_Complete_Roadmap_v5.md §11. On branch claude/gallant-brown-zg0wc1 (a new PR after #6's merge; B3 noted). Owed (its owed: line): the page's first line; the complete edition in every case file; the case-file specification; the method version inside the signed case; the standalone checker; the publication refusal and the warning's sentence; off-the-record attestations; import with recreation; acceptance gated on recreation.

## U41 · QUESTION · 2026-10-05 · session_01JZtUsAKpStQoiwF6rzqsyJ · primary
Bob, 2026-10-05, asked what an evidence engine like Civicsmith can and cannot do with six kinds of material, and directed this question to you ("send it to BOB"). For each area: confirm what is built, state the limits, and propose options for each gap, for Bob to rule on (P17). The design session's reading of the requirements, to check, not to rely on:
1. Time. Deadlines in calendar or business days against the profile's holidays, "undetermined" when a year is missing (action-clocks R1–R12; jurisdictions R26, R33, R43). Limits seen: days are UTC; time zone and office hours stored but unused; R26's extension never read; no recurring-meeting model; no document effective dates extracted; chronology only in the counsel packet.
2. Organisations. A subject registry with fixed kinds and three relations only (proxy_for, member_of, overlaps; entities R1–R8, R26); counterparties by role (jurisdictions R24). Seen missing: "reports to" or "contracts with", who held a role when, an org chart and its history.
3. Law and regulation. Standards declared with citation, issuer, captured text, in-force period and a supersedes chain (standards R1–R8); determinations per standard (conformance). Seen missing: a code → chapter → section structure (each section must be its own standard); amendment history beyond what members declare.
4. Court cases and precedent. "court" is a standard and source kind; filings builds a counsel packet only. Seen missing: tracking a court case (filings, rulings, appeals) and an "interprets" link between a decision and the standard it construes.
5. Calculation. The only calculation is consequences R2 (sum, difference, count, product, ratio over cited figures, weakest-input grade), reachable only after a noncompliant determination; spreadsheets read as text and structure, formulas kept but not evaluated (office-readers R10). Seen missing: a general, reproducible calculation a member can cite while investigating (percentages, budget against actuals) and a budget or financial-report reader. Example: checking a city's claim that it filled 90% of reported potholes from its own service-request export (journey 6 on the design session's journeys page).
6. Plain-language questions. An inquiry can hold any question (UC-047) and a lead can become one; the assistant's question-to-search flow (ASSISTANT-PILOT §2) is designed, not built. Seen missing: anything that helps answer a member's question of meaning, e.g. "What is this sewer maintenance charge on my water bill?", by finding and explaining the authorising ordinance or rate schedule.
Answer on your outbox; the design session takes your answer to Bob with the design side (how members meet each capability, and the screens' honest wording about limits).

DEC-133 (Bob, J8: "agree, including adding the reusable join link that an administrator can enable (and use in the webpage) or not"): joining through the group's own website.
- A website key. An administrator may create one. It serves both of Bob's workflows: an open form, or an application someone approves on the website.

- Website invitations expire after seven days by default, and an unused one can be withdrawn.
- The key stays on the website's server.
- Civicsmith sends no email.
- A one-time warning when a key is created or the link enabled: anyone let through can see the group's shared work.

## U66 · ANSWER · re B54 · 2026-10-06 · session_01SEmEip2dMnNBFgsXF9Tids · primary
DEC-145 (the design session): the members' words for §6B and §6C.
1. Force: "Requires", "Recommends", "Allows"; for a policy, "Required" or "At the discretion of <office>", with "criteria stated" or "no criteria stated", always beside the document's own words.
2. Benchmarks: "Standard · binds <body>" beside "Benchmark · not binding on <body>"; against a benchmark, "slower than" or "below", never "violated" or "nonconforming".
3. Absences: "Cited, not seen" and "Looked for, not found", each distinct from "Nobody looked".
4. A policy kept from the public (K1740): "Not public", with whose it is to release; never "confidential".
5. A silent change: a Noticed item, "Changed without notice: the text differs from the copy captured on <date>, and no amendment was announced".
6. Access: "Free to read", "Reading room only", "Behind a paywall".
7. Discretion: "Discretion used by <office>, <date>", with "stated reason: …" or "no reason stated"; across uses, "a pattern in how discretion is used".
8. "Policy" always carries its owner; the group's own is "our group's policy". "Practice" is not used in Civicsmith's own words.
journeys.html corrected: laws and codes are built; policies and standards as kinds of their own are ruled and planned.

## U81 · QUESTION · 2026-10-06 · session_01SEmEip2dMnNBFgsXF9Tids · primary
Bob asked whether a member may sign in through the hosted Claude Code's own login (no command line on the member's computer), and that the answer be added to the register "accurately and unambiguously with all terms included". I read pages L and A live today (2026-10-06, WebFetch asked to quote verbatim). I do not write `build/`; please add the following to `build/terms/anthropic.md` (ids are the next free ones; renumber if needed) and confirm.

### AT-26
- **Governs:** Claude Code's sign-in (claude.ai accounts: Pro, Max; Team and Enterprise as invited)
- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication ("Log in to Claude Code")
- **Read:** 2026-10-06
- **Text:**
  > If your browser shows a login code instead of redirecting back after you sign in, paste it into the terminal at the `Paste code here if prompted` prompt. This happens when the browser can't reach Claude Code's local callback server, which is common in WSL2, SSH sessions, and containers.
  > You can authenticate with any of these account types:
  > * **Claude Pro or Max subscription**: log in with your claude.ai account. […]
  > * **Claude for Teams or Enterprise**: log in with the claude.ai account your team admin invited you to.
- **Bears on:** Claude Code hosted in a group's container: a member signing in completes Anthropic's sign-in in their own browser and then must enter the login code at the hosted binary's own prompt. Free plans are not among the account types this page lists for Claude Code.

### AT-27
- **Governs:** Claude Code's stored credentials (every account type)
- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication ("Credential management")
- **Read:** 2026-10-06
- **Text:**
  > On Linux, credentials are stored in `~/.claude/.credentials.json` with file mode `0600`.
  > Claude Code manages `.credentials.json` through `/login` and `/logout`.
- **Bears on:** a member's sign-in to hosted Claude Code is written by the binary into the container it runs in (the group's), not by Civicsmith's code.

### U-7 · A member signing in through hosted Claude Code's own login, from a Civicsmith screen
- **Say:**
  - Met: the binary is unmodified (AT-14); the member uses their own subscription, billed to them (AT-15); the sign-in completes on Anthropic's own page (AT-17's "must complete through Anthropic's own flow"); an end user signing in to "the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own Claude subscription, including where a platform hosts Claude Code" is not prevented (AT-18).
  - Mechanics: the member gets a login code in their browser and it must reach the hosted binary's `Paste code here if prompted` prompt (AT-26); the binary stores the result in the container (AT-27).
  - Anthropic's answer to questions like this: "For questions about permitted authentication methods for your use case, please contact sales." (AT-25).
- **Do not say** (each is unsettled by the pages):
  - (a) whether a Civicsmith screen that takes the login code and passes it to the binary "collect[s] … or intermediate[s] Claude.ai credentials or session tokens" (AT-17), or whether carrying the member's keystrokes to the binary's own prompt does;
  - (b) whether a Civicsmith button that starts that sign-in is offering "Claude.ai login into their own applications" (AT-16) or the member "signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary" (AT-18);
  - (c) whether the credential the binary writes in the group's container is the developer "stor[ing]" it (AT-17; as U-2);
  - (d) who agrees to the Commercial Terms as the one "running Claude Code in your products" (AT-14): each group, or Civicsmith's makers (as U-2).
- **Status:** answerable only by Anthropic (AT-25). Bob is asked (layouts page S6) whether to ask.

Please also mark wherever the build state describes the subscription path's setup-token step, if any statement there goes beyond AT-22 and U-2.

- (6) Official local names (offices, laws, programs, places) stay as they are, with an explanation in the member's language. An official translation is used with its source, held in the place's rules. Outward communications go in the office's language, with a labelled translation for the member. The group's own local words are ordinary words.
- Owed (BOB): the grant; the protected set and its second check; the adoption record and undo; the marks; local names and official translations in jurisdictions; outward language; the ops behind `owed:translationgrant`, `owed:translationconfirm`, `owed:translationrevert DEC-157`.

## U88 · NOTICE · 2026-10-06 · session_01SEmEip2dMnNBFgsXF9Tids · primary
DEC-161 (Bob: "I suggest we start with a 1/2 second delay, but if you can find best practices that give us a proven value then we can start there"):
- On hover, an explanation or card shows after the pointer rests 0.5 s; it stays while the pointer is on the thing or on the tip; it closes 0.5 s after the pointer leaves both.
- Keyboard focus and a tap show it at once; Escape closes it; one at a time.
- Evidence: Nielsen Norman Group's timing guidelines for exposing hidden content (wait 0.3–0.5 s; keep it until the pointer has been away for more than 0.5 s); WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.13 (dismissible, hoverable, persistent).
- `.cs-tip` no longer passes the pointer through.
- Owed (BOB): these timings and 1.4.13's conditions in the shell when built.

A budget figure shows "did not move" with its adoption date; a figure no source dates shows "Undetermined" and is never placed by its period; the note points to recording the event on the Timeline with its dating passage. The column sorts by calendar date, undated last. Owed: the money trail view answers each fact's movement date from its concerned event (with that event's date precision), "did not move" for a non-actual phase, and undetermined when no event dates it; the heading's explanation held for translation. PR #13 head c0d38c5a.

## U114 · NOTICE · 2026-10-07 · session_01XZRZG4F5h3eL54heRZyusj · primary
Design detail on PR #13 since U113, from Bob's comment (a means to settle an uncertain value or add to a budget-only line; no ruling): under a table, each uncertain item gets a block stating what is missing and why, what source would settle it, and existing acts only: `owed:findin DEC-164`, `recordfact` (from another source, cited), `createevent` (the movement, with its dating passage), `actioncreate` (ask the office for the record), `acquire` (the office's own copy, raising the capture grade), `gradenote`, `checkrequest` (a member with the right expertise), `hypothesishold` (a member's reading, labelled theirs, never evidence). A budget-only figure is answered by the paid figure read in at its own stage, compared (`reconcile`) and never merged; a later change is an adjustment naming the figure it adjusts (`adjusts`). Settling a gap never changes a figure; what is added is a new fact or event beside it with its own source. The money screen's registry row gains these seven ops. Owed: none beyond the acts' own; the money trail view lists each fact's undetermined or absent dimensions so the view can offer them.

## U115 · NOTICE · 2026-10-07 · session_01XZRZG4F5h3eL54heRZyusj · primary
Wording, on Bob's direction ("'How to settle it' sounds more definitive than it might end up being"; no ruling): where a screen offers ways to resolve something undetermined, it speaks of adding evidence, never of settling it. The money trail's section is "Where more evidence would help"; each block says what evidence would help; the Timeline's undetermined-order notice reads "More evidence could order them". Words added: more evidence may agree or disagree with what is there; where two sources disagree, both stay, shown in tension (the existing mark), and the screen never picks one. The Undetermined mark's explanation reads "the record can't determine this from what your group holds". Owed: these words, held for translation, wherever the product offers acts against an undetermined value (U108, U114).

The calculation screen's registry row gains `testify`; the registry gains the screen `help`. ## U257 · ANSWER · re B217 · 2026-10-14 · session_01BTQuPg86LEUTdpXJuPbtM2 · primary
Re B217 (K3365). The move, as the lane takes it (DEC-257, in progress):
(1) **Scope.** Two halves. (a) The lane's: every member-facing sentence of the 20 L11 modules is keyed in `words.json` — the design's own words, the modules' `words.mjs` rows (DEC-252), affordances and standards (DEC-253), T49's unworded sentences (DEC-256), and now the English still written inline in module source (refusal texts in `refusal(CODE, "…")` calls, composed `says` sentences, notes, labels) — plus, per module, the list of keys it reads (`screens/words-modules.json`'s form, a `reads` list per module) so each START names its keys. (b) The reading mechanism, BOB's to rule (P17, module architecture), the lane's recommendation: `words.json` is generated into the plane at build (bundler), not re-taken by hand: `setup-words`' `WORD_ROWS` stops being a pinned copy (R1–R9's commit chain retired) and becomes the build's output from the merged file; one reader, `word(key, vars)` (setup-words or interface-shell exports it), fills `{placeholders}` and returns the English; a module holds keys only, never English; its tests assert that each key it uses exists in `words.json` and that it calls the reader, never a sentence's text (the `PLAN_WORDS`-style verbatim copies and their tests go). A word change is then one edit to `words.json` and a rebuild: no module job.
(2) **Requirements.** Yes, for every L11 module that quotes English: its requirements name keys instead of sentences (the lane's key lists give them); `setup-words`' R1–R9 are replaced by the generated list and the reader; `journey-check` checks drawn text by key. The lane changes no requirement file (never `build/`); BOB re-words them from the lists.
(3) **When and where.** The inventory of inline English in the 20 modules on `tranche/T49` is running now; keying it, with the T49-path drawn text already being keyed (DEC-257's guard: no screen's unmatched sentences may rise), rides **PR #39**. Target: ready tonight (UTC), a few hours. Because L11 must read keys only PR #39 carries, the lane asks BOB to merge PR #39 into `main` (or `tranche/T49`) as soon as the MERGE entry says ready, before L11's STARTs, rather than at T49's close.
(4) A MERGE entry (re U251) follows when ready, with the per-module key lists' path and counts.

## U287 · NOTICE · 2026-10-15 · session_01HcUUthHRZ9j5aP76mbxgqB · primary
DEC-280 (Bob, 2026-10-15, from the live site): a pressed button answers at once, in four states. Pressed: it sinks while held, keyboard focus shows a ring. Working: from the press until Civicsmith answers, a turning ring and the busy pointer, a second press does nothing, a screen reader hears `shell.working` ("Working…"). Answered: an act of rung Terminal, Signed or Outward stays disabled with a check; any other shows a check about two seconds and is ready again, its result shown where the member is looking. Refused: ready again, the refusal beside it. Sounds (Bob: "C"): each member's own setting, off to start, under Your account › Language and appearance › Sounds; on, a soft click on a press and a low tone on a refusal. The stylesheet carries it (`components.css`: `.cs-btn:active`, `[aria-busy="true"]`, `[data-done]`), so a module needs only to set the attributes.

Folded: `BIO_Interaction_Constructs_v0_1.md` §F (RULED 2026-10-15); `visual-language/components.css` (and `civicsmith.css`); keys `shell.working`, `screen.account.sounds`, `.off`, `.on`, `.help`; Your account's drawing. ## U309 · ANSWER · re B255 · 2026-10-16 · session_01He2uMKmm4ZtziW18jtY1BD · primary
Already ruled: **DEC-254** (Bob's S24, "A", 14 October; `docs/development/DECISIONS.md`), drawn on the screens page since then; N1173 folds it, no new DEC. What the question page draws:

1. **Where the act sits.** The question's third step, "Say what you conclude" (`question.steps.conclude`, DEC-288), in its Concluding section. The choice "The reading you conclude on" lists the readings the team has accepted and, last, **"Write the reading yourself"** (`question.conclude.own.choice`), offered with or without an assistant. Picking it opens one field, **"Your reading"** (`question.conclude.own.field`), help `question.conclude.own.help`: "Say in your own words what the cited passages show. It is marked as yours, not the assistant's, and concluding on it accepts it in the same act." **With no reading to choose** (no assistant, or none accepted), the choice is not drawn: the field "Your reading" stands alone in its place, and the step's empty line is `question.empty.conclude.own` (replacing `question.empty.conclude`): "Nothing to conclude on yet: a conclusion adopts a reading, the assistant's accepted or one you write yourself, and a reading stands on cited passages." Then the same fields as any conclusion: "What would change it" (required), "Nothing could change it", "Commentary", and **Conclude**.
2. **Its claim and its legs.** The claim is the text of "Your reading". Its legs are the passages cited on the question, with the member's own answers in "What each passage carries" ("If this turned out to be wrong, would your answer still hold?", `inquiryground`, "Save the answers"): the same legs, the same strength computation and the same "Your answer fails if any one of these fails" read-back as any reading. Concluding is refused, in plain words, while a cited passage has no answer.
3. **Accept, Stand on, conclude.** No separate Accept: concluding on her own reading accepts it in the same act (DEC-254); there is nothing of someone else's to accept. Once concluded it is a reading like any other: listed under Supports' "Reading shown", and "Stand on this one for {project}…" (`screen.question.reading.standfor`) works on it as on an accepted proposal; publishing follows the same path.
4. **Told apart.** Everywhere the reading shows (Supports, the Concluded record, the case), it carries **"Written by {member}"** (`question.reading.by.member`, with the person mark), never the machine-work mark a proposal carries; drawn in the question's concluded state h ("Concluded by Mai … Written by Mai").

Owed (DEC-254's owed: line, unchanged): the concluding act accepts a member's own reading, marked hers, accepted and concluded in one act, with the same requirements as any conclusion; offered with or without an assistant; inquiry's `NO_CLAIM` reads the new words; `question.empty.conclude.own` replaces `question.empty.conclude`. The words are in the design's `words.json` (`screens/words-t49.src.py` lines 86–89, 112), carried by the design merge (`e074feaa46` or newer).
